That is, each subset must contain '\n        '**at least one element from S1** and **at least one element from S2**.\\n'\n        '\\n'\n        'The list of {M} subsets is as follows:\\n'\n        '{Sets}\\n'\n        '\\n'\n        '**Output Format:** Your final answer should be a single line containing the elements '\n        'of `S1`, separated by spaces. (Subset `S2` is implicitly defined as `S \\\\ S1`.)'\n    )\n\n    def __init__(self, **kwargs):\n        super().__init__(**kwargs)\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    #  Generation\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    def _generate(self) -> None:\n        \"\"\"\n        Create a solvable SetSplitting instance.\n        Strategy: first pick a hidden partition (S1_hidden, S2_hidden),\n        then sample subsets that are consistent with it (each subset\n        includes at least one element from each side).\n        \"\"\"\n        # Reasonable ranges; can be overridden via self.parameter\n        N = self.parameter.get(\"N\", None)\n        M = self.parameter.get(\"M\", None)\n\n        if N is None:\n            N = random.randint(4, 12)\n        if M is None:\n            M = random.randint(3, 15)\n\n        # Build universe S = {0, 1, ..., N-1}\n        universe = list(range(N))\n\n        # Randomly split into two non-empty parts (hidden solution)\n        k = random.randint(1, N - 1)\n        shuffled = random.sample(universe, N)\n        S1_hidden = set(shuffled[:k])\n        S2_hidden = set(shuffled[k:])\n\n        # Generate M subsets, each guaranteed to be \"splittable\"\n        subsets: List[List[int]] = []\n        for _ in range(M):\n            # pick at least one from each side\n            elem_s1 = random.choice(list(S1_hidden))\n            elem_s2 = random.choice(list(S2_hidden))\n            core = {elem_s1, elem_s2}\n            # Optionally add more random elements\n            extras = random.sample(\n                universe, random.randint(0, min(N - 2, N // 2))\n            )\n            subset_set = core | set(extras)\n            subset = sorted(subset_set)\n            subsets.append(subset)\n\n        # Store in parameter dict for prompt generation & scoring\n        self.parameter[\"N\"] = N\n        self.parameter[\"M\"] = M\n        self.parameter[\"subsets\"] = subsets\n        self.parameter[\"S1_hidden\"] = sorted(S1_hidden)\n        self.parameter[\"S2_hidden\"] = sorted(S2_hidden)\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    #  Prompt\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    def _prompt_generate(self) -> str:\n        N = self.parameter[\"N\"]\n        M = self.parameter[\"M\"]\n        subsets = self.parameter[\"subsets\"]\n\n        # Format subsets nicely: one per line, e.g. \"{0, 3, 5}\"\n        lines = []\n        for i, s in enumerate(subsets):\n            set_str = \"{\" + \", \".join(str(x) for x in s) + \"}\"\n            lines.append(f\"  Subset {i}: {set_str}\")\n        sets_block = \"\\n\".join(lines)\n\n        return self.prompt_template.format(\n            N=N,\n            N_minus_1=N - 1,\n            M=M,\n            Sets=sets_block,\n        )\n\n    # match the '1' inside 'S1' or '2' inside 'S2').\n    _INT_RE = re.compile(r'(?<![a-zA-Z])-?\\d+(?![a-zA-Z])')\n\n    def _process(self, answer: Optional[str]) -> Optional[List[int]]:\n        \"\"\"\n        Try to parse the model answer into a list of integers (S1).\n        Returns None if parsing fails.\n        \"\"\"\n        if answer is None:\n            return None\n\n        answer = answer.strip()\n        if not answer:\n            return None\n\n        # Extract all integer tokens that are not part of a word\n        matches = self._INT_RE.findall(answer)\n        if not matches:\n            return None\n\n        parsed = [int(x) for x in matches]\n        return parsed\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    #  Scorer\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    def scorer(self, output: str) -> float:\n        \"\"\"\n        Score in [-1.0, +1.0].\n\n        -1.0 : unparseable answer or completely invalid partition.\n        Partial credit: linearly scaled by fraction of subsets that are\n        properly split, assuming the partition is valid.\n        +1.0 : perfect answer — every subset is split.\n        \"\"\"\n        N = self.parameter[\"N\"]\n        M = self.parameter[\"M\"]\n        subsets = self.parameter[\"subsets\"]\n\n        # Step 1 — parse the answer into S1.\n        # Delegate to processor to handle <answer> tags.\n        parsed = self.processor(output)  # calls _process internally\n\n        if parsed is None or len(parsed) == 0:\n            return -1.0\n\n        # Step 2 — validate the partition\n        S1 = set(parsed)\n        universe = set(range(N))\n\n        # S1 must be a subset of the universe\n        if not S1.issubset(universe):\n            return -1.0\n\n        # Check for duplicates (len difference)\n        if len(parsed) != len(S1):\n            return -0.8  # duplicate elements — slight penalty\n\n        # S2 is implicitly the complement\n        S2 = universe - S1\n\n        # Step 3 — count how many subsets are split\n        split_count = 0\n        for sub in subsets:\n            sub_set = set(sub)\n            has_s1 = bool(sub_set & S1)\n            has_s2 = bool(sub_set & S2)\n            if has_s1 and has_s2:\n                split_count += 1\n\n        # Step 4 — map [0, M] → [-1.0, +1.0]\n        if M == 0:\n            score = 1.0\n        else:\n            score = 2.0 * (split_count / M) - 1.0\n\n        # Clamp to [-1.0, +1.0] for safety\n        score = max(-1.0, min(1.0, score))\n        return score\n","numLines":187,"startLine":1,"totalLines":187}}}

\"\"\"\n    Compute the approximate KL divergence between two distributions.\n    Schulman blog: http://joschu.net/blog/kl-approx.html\n\n    Args:\n        log_probs: Log probabilities of the new The SetSplitting Environment Class (in `Gym/`)\n\nThe environment is registered in `/workspace/Gym/environments/__init__.py`:\n\n```python\nfrom Gym.environments.set_splitting import SetSplitting_Environment\n\nidentifier2environment = {\n    \"SetSplitting\": SetSplitting_Environment,\n}\n```\n\nFull content of `/workspace/Gym/environments/set_splitting/environment.py` (the environment definition and its `scorer()` reward function):\n\n```python\n# SetSplitting environment: partition a ground set into S1, S2 such that\n# every given subset contains at least one element from each side.\n# scorer(output) -> float in [-1.0, +1.0].\n\nimport random\nimport re\nfrom typing import Optional, List, Set\n\nfrom Gym.environment import VerifiableEnvironment\n\n\nclass SetSplitting_Environment(VerifiableEnvironment):\n    prompt_template = (\n        'Define the full set `S` as all {N} integers from `0` to `{N_minus_1}`.\\n'\n        '\\n'\n        'Your task is to partition `S` into two **disjoint subsets** `S1` and `S2` such that:\\n'\n        '- `S1 ∪ S2 = S` and `S1 ∩ S2 = ∅`\\n'\n        '- For each of the following {M} subsets (each a subset of `S`), the subset is '\n        '**not fully contained** in either `S1` or `S2`. That is, each subset must contain '\n        '**at least one element from S1** and **at least one element from S2**.\\n'\n        '\\n'\n        'The list of {M} subsets is as follows:\\n'\n        '{Sets}\\n'\n        '\\n'\n        '**Output Format:** Your final answer should be a single line containing the elements '\n        'of `S1`, separated by spaces. (Subset `S2` is implicitly defined as `S \\\\ S1`.)'\n    )\n\n    def __init__(self, **kwargs):\n        super().__init__(**kwargs)\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    #  Generation\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    def _generate(self) -> None:\n        \"\"\"\n        Create a solvable SetSplitting instance.\n        Strategy: first pick a hidden partition (S1_hidden, S2_hidden),\n        then sample subsets that are consistent with it (each subset\n        includes at least one element from each side).\n        \"\"\"\n        # Reasonable ranges; can be overridden via self.parameter\n        N = self.parameter.get(\"N\", None)\n        M = self.parameter.get(\"M\", None)\n\n        if N is None:\n            N = random.randint(4, 12)\n        if M is None:\n            M = random.randint(3, 15)\n\n        # Build universe S = {0, 1, ..., N-1}\n        universe = list(range(N))\n\n        # Randomly split into two non-empty parts (hidden solution)\n        k = random.randint(1, N - 1)\n        shuffled = random.sample(universe, N)\n        S1_hidden = set(shuffled[:k])\n        S2_hidden = set(shuffled[k:])\n\n        # Generate M subsets, each guaranteed to be \"splittable\"\n        subsets: List[List[int]] = []\n        for _ in range(M):\n            # pick at least one from each side\n            elem_s1 = random.choice(list(S1_hidden))\n            elem_s2 = random.choice(list(S2_hidden))\n            core = {elem_s1, elem_s2}\n            # Optionally add more random elements\n            extras = random.sample(\n                universe, random.randint(0, min(N - 2, N // 2))\n            )\n            subset_set = core | set(extras)\n            subset = sorted(subset_set)\n            subsets.append(subset)\n\n        # Store in parameter dict for prompt generation & scoring\n        self.parameter[\"N\"] = N\n        self.parameter[\"M\"] = M\n        self.parameter[\"subsets\"] = subsets\n        self.parameter[\"S1_hidden\"] = sorted(S1_hidden)\n        self.parameter[\"S2_hidden\"] = sorted(S2_hidden)\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    #  Prompt\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    def _prompt_generate(self) -> str:\n        N = self.parameter[\"N\"]\n        M = self.parameter[\"M\"]\n        subsets = self.parameter[\"subsets\"]\n\n        # Format subsets nicely: one per line, e.g. \"{0, 3, 5}\"\n        lines = []\n        for i, s in enumerate(subsets):\n            set_str = \"{\" + \", \".join(str(x) for x in s) + \"}\"\n            lines.append(f\"  Subset {i}: {set_str}\")\n        sets_block = \"\\n\".join(lines)\n\n        return self.prompt_template.format(\n            N=N,\n            N_minus_1=N - 1,\n            M=M,\n            Sets=sets_block,\n        )\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    #  Process (parse answer)\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    # Pattern: integer not embedded in a larger identifier (e.g. won't\n    # match the '1' inside 'S1' or '2' inside 'S2').\n    _INT_RE = re.compile(r'(?<![a-zA-Z])-?\\d+(?![a-zA-Z])')\n\n    def _process(self, answer: Optional[str]) -> Optional[List[int]]:\n        \"\"\"\n        Try to parse the model answer into a list of integers (S1).\n        Returns None if parsing fails.\n        \"\"\"\n        if answer is None:\n            return None\n\n        answer = answer.strip()\n        if not answer:\n            return None\n\n        # Extract all integer tokens that are not part of a word\n        matches = self._INT_RE.findall(answer)\n        if not matches:\n            return None\n\n        parsed = [int(x) for x in matches]\n        return parsed\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    #  Scorer\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    def scorer(self, output: str) -> float:\n        \"\"\"\n        Score in [-1.0, +1.0].\n\n        -1.0 : unparseable answer or completely invalid partition.\n        Partial credit: linearly scaled by fraction of subsets that are\n        properly split, assuming the partition is valid.\n        +1.0 : perfect answer — every subset is split.\n        \"\"\"\n        N = self.parameter[\"N\"]\n        M = self.parameter[\"M\"]\n        subsets = self.parameter[\"subsets\"]\n\n        # Step 1 — parse the answer into S1.\n        # Delegate to processor to handle <answer> tags.\n        parsed = self.processor(output)  # calls _process internally\n\n        if parsed is None or len(parsed) == 0:\n            return -1.0\n\n        # Step 2 — validate the partition\n        S1 = set(parsed)\n        universe = set(range(N))\n\n        # S1 must be a subset of the universe\n        if not S1.issubset(universe):\n            return -1.0\n\n        # Check for duplicates (len difference)\n        if len(parsed) != len(S1):\n            return -0.8  # duplicate elements — slight penalty\n\n        # S2 is implicitly the complement\n        S2 = universe - S1\n\n        # Step 3 — count how many subsets are split\n        split_count = 0\n        for sub in subsets:\n            sub_set = set(sub)\n            has_s1 = bool(sub_set & S1)\n            has_s2 = bool(sub_set & S2)\n            if has_s1 and has_s2:\n                split_count += 1\n\n        # Step 4 — map [0, M] → [-1.0, +1.0]\n        if M == 0:\n            score = 1.0\n        else:\n            score = 2.0 * (split_count / M) - 1.0\n\n        # Clamp to [-1.0, +1.0] for safety\n        score = max(-1.0, min(1.0, score))\n        return score\n```\n\n---\n\n## 3. The `rlve.sh` Training Script\n\nFull content of `/workspace/scripts/training/Qwen2.5-0.5B-Instruct/rlve.sh`:\n\n```bash\n#!/bin/bash\n\nif [ $# -lt 3 ]; then\n    echo \"Usage: $0 WANDB_PROJECT RUN_NAME ENVIRONMENT_LIST [EXTRA_TRAIN_ARGS...]\"\n    exit 1\nfi\n\n# BENCH: locked training task types for this workspace — do not change names.\nLOCKED_ENVIRONMENT_LIST=\"SetSplitting\"\n\nWANDB_PROJECT=$1\nRUN_NAME=$2\nENVIRONMENT_LIST=\"${LOCKED_ENVIRONMENT_LIST}\"\nENVIRONMENT_LIST=\"${LOCKED_ENVIRONMENT_LIST}\"\nshift 3\nEXTRA_ARGS=(\"$@\")\n\n# NOTE: This key is hardcoded at user request for quick launch.\n# For better security, prefer exporting WANDB_API_KEY in shell/secret manager instead.\n: \"${WANDB_API_KEY:?Set WANDB_API_KEY in the runtime environment}\"\n# Legacy global cleanup mode is now opt-in only.\n# Default behavior avoids killing unrelated runs in the same container.\nRLVE_ENABLE_GLOBAL_CLEANUP=\"${RLVE_ENABLE_GLOBAL_CLEANUP:-0}\"\necho \"RLVE_ENABLE_GLOBAL_CLEANUP=${RLVE_ENABLE_GLOBAL_CLEANUP}\"\n\nif [ \"${RLVE_ENABLE_GLOBAL_CLEANUP}\" = \"1\" ]; then\n    echo \"RLVE_ENABLE_GLOBAL_CLEANUP=1: running legacy global cleanup.\"\n    pkill -9 sglang || true\n    sleep 3\n    ray stop --force || true\n    pkill -9 ray || true\n    pkill -9 python || true\n    sleep 3\n    pkill -9 ray || true\n    pkill -9 python || true\nelse\n    echo \"Skip global cleanup (default=0). current=${VISIBLE_GPU_SIGNATURE}.\"\n            echo \"ERROR: Set RLVE_RAY_DASHBOARD_URL to another port (or stop old Ray runtime) and retry.\"\n            exit 1\n        fi\n    elif [ \"${RLVE_ALLOW_REUSE_WITHOUT_META}\" != \"1\" ]; then\n        echo \"ERROR: Found Ray runtime at ${RAY_DASHBOARD_URL}, but metadata ${RAY_RUNTIME_META_FILE} is missing.\"\n        echo \"ERROR: To avoid unsafe reuse, set RLVE_RAY_DASHBOARD_URL to a fresh port and retry.\"\n        echo \"ERROR: Or set RLVE_ALLOW_REUSE_WITHOUT_META=1 to bypass this check.\"\n        exit 1\n    else\n        echo \"WARN: Reusing runtime without metadata check (RLVE_ALLOW_REUSE_WITHOUT_META=1).\"\n    fi\nelse\n    echo \"No active Ray runtime detected; starting a new head node on port ${RAY_DASHBOARD_PORT}.\"\n    ray start --head \\\n        --node-ip-address \"${MASTER_ADDR}\" \\\n        --num-gpus \"${NUM_GPUS}\" \\\n        --port \"${RAY_GCS_PORT}\" \\\n        --dashboard-host=0.0.0.0 \\\n        --dashboard-port \"${RAY_DASHBOARD_PORT}\" \\\n        --ray-client-server-port \"${RAY_CLIENT_SERVER_PORT}\" \\\n        --metrics-export-port \"${RAY_METRICS_EXPORT_PORT}\" \\\n        --node-manager-port \"${RAY_NODE_MANAGER_PORT}\" \\\n        --object-manager-port \"${RAY_OBJECT_MANAGER_PORT}\" \\\n        --dashboard-agent-listen-port \"${RAY_DASHBOARD_AGENT_LISTEN_PORT}\" \\\n        --dashboard-agent-grpc-port \"${RAY_DASHBOARD_AGENT_GRPC_PORT}\" \\\n        --runtime-env-agent-port \"${RAY_RUNTIME_ENV_AGENT_PORT}\" \\\n        --min-worker-port \"${RAY_MIN_WORKER_PORT}\" \\\n        --max-worker-port \"${RAY_MAX_WORKER_PORT}\" \\\n        --temp-dir \"${RAY_TEMP_DIR}\" \\\n        --disable-usage-stats\n    cat > \"${RAY_RUNTIME_META_FILE}\" <<EOF\nRAY_RUNTIME_META_VERSION=${RAY_RUNTIME_META_VERSION}\nVISIBLE_GPU_SIGNATURE=${VISIBLE_GPU_SIGNATURE}\nNUM_GPUS=${NUM_GPUS}\nRAY_GCS_PORT=${RAY_GCS_PORT}\nRAY_DASHBOARD_URL=${RAY_DASHBOARD_URL}\nRAY_DASHBOARD_PORT=${RAY_DASHBOARD_PORT}\nRAY_CLIENT_SERVER_PORT=${RAY_CLIENT_SERVER_PORT}\nRAY_METRICS_EXPORT_PORT=${RAY_METRICS_EXPORT_PORT}\nRAY_NODE_MANAGER_PORT=${RAY_NODE_MANAGER_PORT}\nRAY_OBJECT_MANAGER_PORT=${RAY_OBJECT_MANAGER_PORT}\nRAY_DASHBOARD_AGENT_LISTEN_PORT=${RAY_DASHBOARD_AGENT_LISTEN_PORT}\nRAY_DASHBOARD_AGENT_GRPC_PORT=${RAY_DASHBOARD_AGENT_GRPC_PORT}\nRAY_RUNTIME_ENV_AGENT_PORT=${RAY_RUNTIME_ENV_AGENT_PORT}\nRAY_MIN_WORKER_PORT=${RAY_MIN_WORKER_PORT}\nRAY_MAX_WORKER_PORT=${RAY_MAX_WORKER_PORT}\nRAY_TEMP_DIR=${RAY_TEMP_DIR}\nEOF\nfi\n\n# Build the runtime environment JSON with proper variable substitution\nRUNTIME_ENV_JSON=\"{\n  \\\"env_vars\\\": {\n    \\\"PYTHONPATH\\\": \\\"/root/Megatron-LM/\\\",\n only disabled by `--disable-rewards-normalization`, which is NOT passed)\n- `grpo_std_normalization = True` (default; only disabled by `--disable-grpo-std-normalization`, which is NOT passed; stays True because `n_samples_per_prompt=8 != 1`)\n\nThe live logs show the effect:\n```\nrollout/raw_reward': -1.0      rollout/rewards': 0.0\nrollout/raw_reward': -0.9643   rollout/rewards': -6.5e-08\nrollout/raw_reward': -0.9875   rollout/rewards': 1.66e-07\nrollout/raw_reward': -0.70625  rollout/rewards': 2.14e-08\n```\nThe `raw_reward` is the raw SetSplitting score in `[-1, 1]`; the normalized `rewards` (used for training) are the GRPO group mean-subtracted / std-divided values (near zero).\n\n### C. Advantage normalization is DISABLED for this run\n`/workspace/slime/backends/megatron_utils/loss.py` lines 161-206: `compute_advantages_and_returns()` only whitens advantages when `args.normalize_advantages` is set. KL reward shaping is disabled\n`--kl-coef` defaults to `0.00` (`/workspace/slime/utils/arguments.py` line 512), and the rlve.sh script does not enable it, so no KL penalty is subtracted from rewards.\n\n### Summary of reward pipeline for SetSplitting\n1. `rlve_rm()` → `problem.verifier(response)` → `scorer()` returns raw score in `[-1, +1]`.\n2. `verifier()` wraps it as `{\"reward\": score, \"accuracy\": ..., \"format_score\": ...}`.\n3. `async_rm()` in `slime/rollout/rm_hub/__init__.py` returns that dict as `sample.reward`.\n4. `post_process_rewards()` in `slime/ray/buffer.py` **normalizes the `\"reward\"` values per GRPO group** (subtract group mean, divide by group std) before training. That is, each subset must contain '\n        '**at least one element from S1** and **at least one element from S2**.\\n'\n        '\\n'\n        'The list of {M} subsets is as follows:\\n'\n        '{Sets}\\n'\n        '\\n'\n        '**Output Format:** Your final answer should be a single line containing the elements '\n        'of `S1`, separated by spaces. (Subset `S2` is implicitly defined as `S \\\\ S1`.)'\n    )\n\n    def __init__(self, **kwargs):\n        super().__init__(**kwargs)\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    #  Generation\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    def _generate(self) -> None:\n        \"\"\"\n        Create a solvable SetSplitting instance.\n        Strategy: first pick a hidden partition (S1_hidden, S2_hidden),\n        then sample subsets that are consistent with it (each subset\n        includes at least one element from each side).\n        \"\"\"\n        # Reasonable ranges; can be overridden via self.parameter\n        N = self.parameter.get(\"N\", None)\n        M = self.parameter.get(\"M\", None)\n\n        if N is None:\n            N = random.randint(4, 12)\n        if M is None:\n            M = random.randint(3, 15)\n\n        # Build universe S = {0, 1, ..., N-1}\n        universe = list(range(N))\n\n        # Randomly split into two non-empty parts (hidden solution)\n        k = random.randint(1, N - 1)\n        shuffled = random.sample(universe, N)\n        S1_hidden = set(shuffled[:k])\n        S2_hidden = set(shuffled[k:])\n\n        # Generate M subsets, each guaranteed to be \"splittable\"\n        subsets: List[List[int]] = []\n        for _ in range(M):\n            # pick at least one from each side\n            elem_s1 = random.choice(list(S1_hidden))\n            elem_s2 = random.choice(list(S2_hidden))\n            core = {elem_s1, elem_s2}\n            # Optionally add more random elements\n            extras = random.sample(\n                universe, random.randint(0, min(N - 2, N // 2))\n            )\n            subset_set = core | set(extras)\n            subset = sorted(subset_set)\n            subsets.append(subset)\n\n        # Store in parameter dict for prompt generation & scoring\n        self.parameter[\"N\"] = N\n        self.parameter[\"M\"] = M\n        self.parameter[\"subsets\"] = subsets\n        self.parameter[\"S1_hidden\"] = sorted(S1_hidden)\n        self.parameter[\"S2_hidden\"] = sorted(S2_hidden)\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    #  Prompt\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    def _prompt_generate(self) -> str:\n        N = self.parameter[\"N\"]\n        M = self.parameter[\"M\"]\n        subsets = self.parameter[\"subsets\"]\n\n        # Format subsets nicely: one per line, e.g. \"{0, 3, 5}\"\n        lines = []\n        for i, s in enumerate(subsets):\n            set_str = \"{\" + \", \".join(str(x) for x in s) + \"}\"\n            lines.append(f\"  Subset {i}: {set_str}\")\n        sets_block = \"\\n\".join(lines)\n\n        return self.prompt_template.format(\n            N=N,\n            N_minus_1=N - 1,\n            M=M,\n            Sets=sets_block,\n        )\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    #  Process (parse answer)\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    # Pattern: integer not embedded in a larger identifier (e.g. won't\n    # match the '1' inside 'S1' or '2' inside 'S2').\n    _INT_RE = re.compile(r'(?<![a-zA-Z])-?\\d+(?![a-zA-Z])')\n\n    def _process(self, answer: Optional[str]) -> Optional[List[int]]:\n        \"\"\"\n        Try to parse the model answer into a list of integers (S1).\n        Returns None if parsing fails.\n        \"\"\"\n        if answer is None:\n            return None\n\n        answer = answer.strip()\n        if not answer:\n            return None\n\n        # Extract all integer tokens that are not part of a word\n        matches = self._INT_RE.findall(answer)\n        if not matches:\n            return None\n\n        parsed = [int(x) for x in matches]\n        return parsed\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    #  Scorer\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    def scorer(self, output: str) -> float:\n        \"\"\"\n        Score in [-1.0, +1.0].\n\n        -1.0 : unparseable answer or completely invalid partition.\n        Partial credit: linearly scaled by fraction of subsets that are\n        properly split, assuming the partition is valid.\n        +1.0 : perfect answer — every subset is split.\n        \"\"\"\n        N = self.parameter[\"N\"]\n        M = self.parameter[\"M\"]\n        subsets = self.parameter[\"subsets\"]\n\n        # Step 1 — parse the answer into S1.\n        # Delegate to processor to handle <answer> tags.\n        parsed = self.processor(output)  # calls _process internally\n\n        if parsed is None or len(parsed) == 0:\n            return -1.0\n\n        # Step 2 — validate the partition\n        S1 = set(parsed)\n        universe = set(range(N))\n\n        # S1 must be a subset of the universe\n        if not S1.issubset(universe):\n            return -1.0\n\n        # Check for duplicates (len difference)\n        if len(parsed) != len(S1):\n            return -0.8  # duplicate elements — slight penalty\n\n        # S2 is implicitly the complement\n        S2 = universe - S1\n\n        # Step 3 — count how many subsets are split\n        split_count = 0\n        for sub in subsets:\n            sub_set = set(sub)\n            has_s1 = bool(sub_set & S1)\n            has_s2 = bool(sub_set & S2)\n            if has_s1 and has_s2:\n                split_count += 1\n\n        # Step 4 — map [0, M] → [-1.0, +1.0]\n        if M == 0:\n            score = 1.0\n        else:\n            score = 2.0 * (split_count / M) - 1.0\n\n        # Clamp to [-1.0, +1.0] for safety\n        score = max(-1.0, min(1.0, score))\n        return score\n```\n\n---\n\n## 3. then\n    echo \"Reusing existing Ray runtime at ${RAY_DASHBOARD_URL}\"\n    if [ -f \"${RAY_RUNTIME_META_FILE}\" ]; then\n        PREV_RAY_RUNTIME_META_VERSION=$(awk -F= '/^RAY_RUNTIME_META_VERSION=/{print $2}' \"${RAY_RUNTIME_META_FILE}\")\n        PREV_VISIBLE_GPU_SIGNATURE=$(awk -F= '/^VISIBLE_GPU_SIGNATURE=/{print $2}' \"${RAY_RUNTIME_META_FILE}\")\n        PREV_RAY_GCS_PORT=$(awk -F= '/^RAY_GCS_PORT=/{print $2}' \"${RAY_RUNTIME_META_FILE}\")\n        if [ \"${PREV_RAY_RUNTIME_META_VERSION:-1}\" != \"${RAY_RUNTIME_META_VERSION}\" ]; then\n            echo \"ERROR: Ray runtime metadata version mismatch on ${RAY_DASHBOARD_URL}.\"\n            echo \"ERROR: existing_meta_version=${PREV_RAY_RUNTIME_META_VERSION:-1}, required_meta_version=${RAY_RUNTIME_META_VERSION}.\"\n            echo \"ERROR: This runtime was started without isolated Ray agent ports. Use a fresh Ray runtime for this GPU set.\"\n            exit 1\n        fi\n        if [ -n \"${PREV_RAY_GCS_PORT}\" ] && [ \"${PREV_RAY_GCS_PORT}\" != \"${RAY_GCS_PORT}\" ]; then\n            echo \"ERROR: Ray runtime GCS port mismatch on ${RAY_DASHBOARD_URL}.\"\n            echo \"ERROR: existing_gcs_port=${PREV_RAY_GCS_PORT}, current_gcs_port=${RAY_GCS_PORT}.\"\n            echo \"ERROR: Set RLVE_RAY_DASHBOARD_URL / RLVE_RAY_GCS_PORT consistently and retry.\"\n            exit 1\n        fi\n        if [ -n \"${PREV_VISIBLE_GPU_SIGNATURE}\" ] && [ \"${PREV_VISIBLE_GPU_SIGNATURE}\" != \"${VISIBLE_GPU_SIGNATURE}\" ]; then\n            echo \"ERROR: Ray runtime GPU signature mismatch on ${RAY_DASHBOARD_URL}.\"\n            echo \"ERROR: existing=${PREV_VISIBLE_GPU_SIGNATURE}, current=${VISIBLE_GPU_SIGNATURE}.\"\n            echo \"ERROR: Set RLVE_RAY_DASHBOARD_URL to another port (or stop old Ray runtime) and retry.\"\n            exit 1\n        fi\n    elif [ \"${RLVE_ALLOW_REUSE_WITHOUT_META}\" != \"1\" ]; then\n        echo \"ERROR: Found Ray runtime at ${RAY_DASHBOARD_URL}, but metadata ${RAY_RUNTIME_META_FILE} is missing.\"\n        echo \"ERROR: To avoid unsafe reuse, set RLVE_RAY_DASHBOARD_URL to a fresh port and retry.\"\n        echo \"ERROR: Or set RLVE_ALLOW_REUSE_WITHOUT_META=1 to bypass this check.\"\n        exit 1\n    else\n        echo \"WARN: Reusing runtime without metadata check (RLVE_ALLOW_REUSE_WITHOUT_META=1).\"\n    fi\nelse\n    echo \"No active Ray runtime detected; starting a new head node on port ${RAY_DASHBOARD_PORT}.\"\n    ray start --head \\\n        --node-ip-address \"${MASTER_ADDR}\" \\\n        --num-gpus \"${NUM_GPUS}\" \\\n        --port \"${RAY_GCS_PORT}\" \\\n        --dashboard-host=0.0.0.0 \\\n        --dashboard-port \"${RAY_DASHBOARD_PORT}\" \\\n        --ray-client-server-port \"${RAY_CLIENT_SERVER_PORT}\" \\\n        --metrics-export-port \"${RAY_METRICS_EXPORT_PORT}\" \\\n        --node-manager-port \"${RAY_NODE_MANAGER_PORT}\" \\\n        --object-manager-port \"${RAY_OBJECT_MANAGER_PORT}\" \\\n        --dashboard-agent-listen-port \"${RAY_DASHBOARD_AGENT_LISTEN_PORT}\" \\\n        --dashboard-agent-grpc-port \"${RAY_DASHBOARD_AGENT_GRPC_PORT}\" \\\n        --runtime-env-agent-port \"${RAY_RUNTIME_ENV_AGENT_PORT}\" \\\n Reward Scaling / Clipping / Normalization Affecting SetSplitting\n\nI searched the entire codebase for reward scaling, clipping, and normalization. Here are the relevant findings:\n\n### A. SetSplitting's own scorer already clamps to `[-1.0, +1.0]`\nIn `/workspace/Gym/environments/set_splitting/environment.py` lines 184-185:\n```python\n# Clamp to [-1.0, +1.0] for safety\nscore = max(-1.0, min(1.0, score))\n```\nThe base class `VerifiableEnvironment.verifier()` also asserts `-1.0 <= score <= +1.0` (`/workspace/Gym/environment.py` line 179).\n\n### B. GRPO group reward normalization is ACTIVE (this is the important one)\n`/workspace/slime/ray/buffer.py` lines 89-114 — `post_process_rewards()`:\n```python\ndef post_process_rewards(self, samples):\n    if self.custom_reward_post_process_func is not None:\n        return self.custom_reward_post_process_func(self.args, samples)\n\n    raw_rewards = [sample.get_reward_value(self.args) for sample in samples]\n    if (\n        self.args.advantage_estimator in [\"grpo\", \"gspo\", \"reinforce_plus_plus_baseline\"]\n        and self.args.rewards_normalization\n    ):\n        # group norm\n        rewards = torch.tensor(raw_rewards, dtype=torch.float)\n        if rewards.shape[-1] == self.args.n_samples_per_prompt * self.args.rollout_batch_size:\n            rewards = rewards.reshape(-1, self.args.n_samples_per_prompt)\n        else:\n            rewards = rewards.view(-1, rewards.shape[-1])\n        mean = rewards.mean(dim=-1, keepdim=True)\n        rewards = rewards - mean\n\n        if self.args.advantage_estimator in [\"grpo\", \"gspo\"] and self.args.grpo_std_normalization:\n            std = rewards.std(dim=-1, keepdim=True)\n            rewards = rewards / (std + 1e-6)\n\n        return raw_rewards, rewards.flatten().tolist()\n\n    return raw_rewards, raw_rewards\n```\n\n**Defaults confirmed from `logs/fix_run/run_20260715_175713/ray_job.log`:**\n- `rewards_normalization = True` (default; only disabled by `--disable-rewards-normalization`, which is NOT passed)\n- `grpo_std_normalization = True` (default; only disabled by `--disable-grpo-std-normalization`, which is NOT passed; stays True because `n_samples_per_prompt=8 != 1`)\n\nThe live logs show the effect:\n```\nrollout/raw_reward': -1.0      rollout/rewards': 0.0\nrollout/raw_reward': -0.9643   rollout/rewards': -6.5e-08\nrollout/raw_reward': -0.9875   rollout/rewards': 1.66e-07\nrollout/raw_reward': -0.70625  rollout/rewards': 2.14e-08\n```\nThe `raw_reward` is the raw SetSplitting score in `[-1, 1]`; the normalized `rewards` (used for training) are the GRPO group mean-subtracted / std-divided values (near zero).\n\n### C. Advantage normalization is DISABLED for this run\n`/workspace/slime/backends/megatron_utils/loss.py` lines 161-206: `compute_advantages_and_returns()` only whitens advantages when `args.normalize_advantages` is set. The rlve.sh script does NOT pass `--normalize-advantages`, so this is off. (Note: `--normalize-advantages` defaults to `False`, per `/workspace/slime/utils/arguments.py` line 568.)\n\n### D. A separate rollout_buffer plugin also normalizes rewards (NOT in the main training path)\n`/workspace/slime_plugins/rollout_buffer/generator/base_generator.py` lines 301-328 defines `normalize_group_data()`, which does GRPO-style `(r - mean) / (std + epsilon)` normalization. This is part of the standalone `rollout_buffer` plugin example (`slime_plugins/rollout_buffer/`), which is not wired into the `train.py`/`rlve.sh` main path (it is loaded only via `rollout_buffer_example.py` / custom `--rollout-buffer-url`). It is NOT used for SetSplitting in the standard training run.\n\n### E. KL reward shaping is disabled\n`--kl-coef` defaults to `0.00` (`/workspace/slime/utils/arguments.py` line 512), and the rlve.sh script does not enable it, so no KL penalty is subtracted from rewards.\n\n### Summary of reward pipeline for SetSplitting\n1. `rlve_rm()` → `problem.verifier(response)` → `scorer()` returns raw score in `[-1, +1]`.\n2. `verifier()` wraps it as `{\"reward\": score, \"accuracy\": ..., \"format_score\": ...}`.\n3. `async_rm()` in `slime/rollout/rm_hub/__init__.py` returns that dict as `sample.reward`.\n4. `post_process_rewards()` in `slime/ray/buffer.py` **normalizes the `\"reward\"` values per GRPO group** (subtract group mean, divide by group std) before training. The raw scores are preserved as `raw_reward`.\n5. `compute_advantages_and_returns()` in `slime/backends/megatron_utils/loss.py` broadcasts the normalized reward as GRPO returns/advantages (no additional whitening since `--normalize-advantages` is off).\n\nThere is no explicit \"reward scaling\" multiplier and no explicit reward clipping beyond the environment's own `[-1, +1]` clamp; the only transformation that materially changes the training signal is the GRPO group normalization in `buffer.py`."}],"totalDurationMs":774371,"totalTokens":83084,"totalToolUseCount":46,"usage":{"input_tokens":5206,"cache_creation_input_tokens":0,"cache_read_input_tokens":64000,"output_tokens":13878,"service_tier":"standard"}}}
